- evidence refresh + first-slice landing note (2026-10-11, run b171537d4e3d4e1ebe8904ee2ccdb1fb, roadmap 61636bb689b747d98e0f793745a844a0, consuming assess 785b4707 @dbc5b76): the export-lane slice landed with run c23d7ee8 (is_bidi_format_control, crates/agenttrace-core/src/statusline.rs:303-311, bounds pinned by rm936_is_bidi_format_control_bounds) — but (a) its doc comment OVERCLAIMS the set: "Cf that reorder or disguise adjacent rendered text" while the predicate matches only the directional subset (U+061C, U+200E-200F, U+202A-202E, U+2066-2069); non-directional Cf spoofing characters U+FEFF, U+2060, U+00AD (exclusion test-pinned), U+180E ride raw through every export lane, the JSON carve-out (main.rs:1113-1128), otel (otel.rs:124-133) — either narrow the doc to "directional" or widen the set; and (b) the interactive TUI display lane missed the neutralization entirely: zero sanitization in crates/agenttrace-tui/src/* (no is_control/FFFD refs), session names derive from the first real user request (pinned session_name_uses_first_real_user_request, lib.rs:3125) and flow raw into rows — short(&session.name, 56) at explorer.rs:989, renders at app.rs:1371, explorer.rs:1102/1142/1208; short() (filters.rs:379) is width-only truncation; ratatui-core 0.1.2 drops Cc only (buffer.rs:351 char::is_control) so the Cf directional set passes into cells and rides raw to the terminal where the emulator's bidi algorithm reorders adjacent column text (name vs cost/model) — the same spoofing class the export lane closed. Fix shape stays this row's acceptance: filter at the name/content boundary with is_bidi_format_control, then extend to the full Cf set per (a).
- EXECUTED (2026-10-11, run b171537d4e3d4e1ebe8904ee2ccdb1fb, implement e7beab2392954ded8afa48bab337ee9e @dbc5b76, pending commit gate): slice 2 landed — the TUI display lane neutralizes at the boundary: sanitize_display (crates/agenttrace-tui/src/filters.rs) filters through agenttrace_core::is_bidi_format_control via the EXISTING path dep (no new dependency, predicate bytes unchanged) and is wired as the choke point INSIDE short() (covers the 10 width-shaped lanes transitively: explorer rows, presentation tables) plus explicit wraps at the 5 raw format! sites (explorer.rs context/size/spend/tool cards, presentation.rs Name line). This closes the row's disputed-evidence note first-hand at the cell level (Cf passes a Cc-only buffer filter — the static read was right): 4 tests pin the directional set stripped char-by-char, honest text (CJK/emoji) verbatim, width-neutrality (zero-width set, width math unchanged), and the short() choke point. The doc-comment overclaim from the rider is closed the NARROW way (doc now names the directional subset exactly and the non-directional members left out). STILL OPEN on this row: the non-directional Cf widening (U+FEFF/U+2060/U+00AD/U+180E) through export lanes and the U+FFFD-replacement acceptance arm — sized as the next slice; JSON carve-out (main.rs:1113-1128) and otel lane stay raw by their recorded design decisions.

- acceptance: staged, each independently shippable: (a) doctor's walk routes through the caching finder and every successful parse persists (store_session + save_session_cache), so an immediate rerun on the same -d corpus reports reusable entries > 0 and strictly lower wall time than the cold run; (b) a per-root wall-clock budget with honest partial-scan disclosure ('scanned N of M roots in Ts') keeps doctor's output valid and rc 0 under truncation instead of a silent multi-minute stall; (c) progress output on stderr before the first parse so a hung run is diagnosable; (d) roots over a stated size get a count-sampled or size-capped pass for the parseable/failed columns (doctor needs counts, not attribution). Regression: a fixture pins that a budget-interrupted run still reports honest partial counts
- evidence: campaign-recorded in agenttrace ROADMAP.md

### Disclose magnitude clamps and flag implausible token magnitudes as anomalies
- id: `rm-726` | track: correctness | priority: 82.0 | status: done
- signals: correctness.silent_magnitude_clamp:crates/agenttrace-core/src/parser.rs::L4929-4950 (number_as_i64 maps u64 payloads above i64::MAX onto i64 bounds and negative JSON numbers to 0 BY DESIGN, recording no clamp fact) + :L1208 (saturating_add on the session accumulator) — downstream pricing multiplies the clamped magnitudes at real per-token rates, so a hostile or corrupted journal renders $42.7T with HEALTH 100 and 'No anomalies detected' (live PoC 2026-10-07, run 32f3b7a1 assess 1b24e1ee @e9e8fd9: usage {input_tokens: 1e19, output_tokens: 1e18} → --sessions cost 42670116110564.3281, tokens 9223372036854775807, anomalies 0; --overview 'Total estimated cost: $42.7T'; --diagnostics 'No anomalies detected'). The disclosure infrastructure exists and is unwired to this path: subtract_cached_input's clamped flag (parser.rs:1228) feeds clamped_records (parser.rs:1053) for the cache-relation domain only; the magnitude domain records nothing. External yardstick: AgentMeasure's conformance-pack healthcheck HC-06 'token stability' (pack stable through v0.5.0, 2026-10-03) treats implausible magnitudes as a first-class violation; upstream luoyuctl #316 (merged 2026-10-06) ships the same clamp family silently — the ecosystem clamps but does not disclose.
- acceptance: (1) every magnitude clamp in number_as_i64 increments a disclosure counter surfaced through the existing clamped_records/disclosure channel, so the run reports how many values were clamped; (2) diagnostics grows an implausible-magnitude anomaly class (any clamped usage field, or a per-message token magnitude above a documented plausibility bound) — the PoC corpus must yield anomalies ≥ 1 and HEALTH < 100 instead of 100; (3) pricing still prices what was parsed (no silent drop) but --overview/--audit disclose the clamp count beside the total; (4) red-first fixtures from the assess PoC corpus (big.jsonl 1e19/1e18; neg.jsonl negative usage → clamp-to-0) pinned BEFORE the fix showing anomalies 0, AFTER showing ≥ 1 with the counter; (5) full battery green; conductor validation digest validation:v1:<sha> recorded in the shipping PR.
- evidence: assess 1b24e1ee result JSON + PoC corpora at /tmp/at-assess-32f3/sandbox (big.jsonl, neg.jsonl); parser.rs:4929-4950/:1208/:1228/:1053 read live at e9e8fd9; research e4913420 dossier (AgentMeasure HC-06 mapping, upstream #316 corroboration); rm-053's evidence-refresh line above binds this corpus into that row's fixture pack.
- notes: minted 2026-10-07 (run 32f3b7a1 roadmap 39e82da1 @ base e9e8fd9 porcelain 0; live fleet census: max band rm-722..725 = ad1e1dcc, next free rm-726 — claimed here, first use); title-checked against every live band and this wall: rm-053 owns the conformance HARNESS, this row owns the defect+fix; sibling negative-input clamps (rm-446 family) are the values-below-zero polarity, this is the above-i64 polarity.
- lead selection (2026-10-07, run 32f3b7a1 prioritize 7606fce2 @e9e8fd9, uncommitted): SELECTED as cycle-3 lead. Rationale: silent-wrong-number disclosure is the fork's core identity; PoC fixtures already exist (big.jsonl/neg.jsonl red-first-able NOW); infra exists unwired (clamped_records :1053); surfaces parser.rs + diagnostics.rs are UNCONTESTED across every live sibling worktree (census 2026-10-07 ~05:4xZ: parser.rs siblings = 2f02ecafb267 journal-rider +2 lines, 36c9140f2603 codex service_tier pricing — both disjoint hunks; diagnostics.rs untouched fleet-wide). - id: `rm-980` | track: correctness | priority: 78.0 | status: done (added 2026-10-07 as campaign-local rm-685, run 2f02ecafb2674e529f662f7cc27162f4 repository-maintenance 78307520 cycle 2 roadmap 13b439bc @518170a; assess 1177e59f F1+F5+F2; flipped done at that run's commit gate 2026-10-08 — review a149cf61 APPROVED, final validation 2a601342 PASS; renumbered rm-685→rm-980 at its landing integration 2026-10-11, conflict case edb878bb27db418fbb13184efccaa721 — the landed wall's rm-685 'transcript-move identity' row holds the numeral, landed-first precedence)
- signals: correctness.codex_seen_value_equality:crates/agenttrace-core/src/parser.rs::L3248-3262 — CodexTotals.seen is a HashSet<Value> keyed on VALUE equality, so a post-compaction window whose running total is value-equal to an earlier window is silently dropped (PoC codex-seen-collision.jsonl, totals 300→1200→300 each with last_token_usage: reported in=1200/out=400; truth 1500/500 — window 3's last {300,100} is fresh billed usage); correctness.codex_same_last_double_count:parser.rs::L3255 — the fresh-only guard has no repeated-last-window protection, so two consecutive windows carrying an identical last_token_usage each contribute it (PoC codex-same-last.jsonl, totals {100,50}→{200,80} with last pinned {100,50}: reported 200/100; truth 200/80); reliability.codex_seen_unbounded:parser.rs::L3305 — the seen set grows with every distinct value for the session's lifetime, so parse memory scales with journal size, not turn count (PoC: 89MiB journal w/ 400k distinct totals → VmHWM 661 MB vs 105 MB control, ~1.4KB/entry)

### Cap and sanitize journal-derived disclosure-counter keys (rm-542 residual)
- id: `rm-594` | track: reliability | priority: 76.0 | status: in_progress
- acceptance: counter keys derived from journal strings are length-capped at the mint sites (first N chars + short hash of the remainder, N pinned by a unit test) so any single hostile or corrupt line contributes O(1) key bytes; red-first regression drives a ~1MB unknown type through parse and asserts (a) the emitted key/report line is byte-bounded by the stated bound, (b) the persisted cache entry stays bounded, (c) the truncated shape still identifies the original (prefix + hash) — visible, not silent; the cap composes with sanitization so no raw control byte survives into a key (ties to rm-595's render arm); no behavior change for real wire shapes (all observed real types are short); SESSION_CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION bump per the rm-230 convention if the persisted key shape changes
- evidence: campaign-recorded in agenttrace ROADMAP.md
- residual corroboration (2026-10-07, run e88f2da2 assess 79d82fc1 C1 at 92149bd): the 12-hex length cap does not bound CARDINALITY — a hostile session_meta line with 150k DISTINCT unknown usage keys parses clean but drives ~96 MB RSS, --doctor text 5.4 MB / --overview text 5.7 MB (95k distinct usage_unknown_key: lines), and a ~4.5 MB persisted cache entry re-parsed on every warm run (hostile corpus /home/agent/.hermes/conductor-delegate-spool/delegate/79d82fc17d62423aad76a0c11ad143bf-scratch/poc-mirror/hostile.jsonl); a future hardening lane would cap distinct-key disclosure counts, not just per-key length — same shape as sibling 9ab0afad's F4 on this base

### cost_audit must not re-price recorded-cost sessions into false drift
- id: `rm-570` | track: reliability | priority: 74.0 | status: implemented (flipped 2026-10-08, run e88f2da2 cycle 3 compound 884f0594; done-flip reserved to the commit gate)
- acceptance: cost_audit distinguishes recorded-cost sessions from estimate-only sessions — for recorded-basis sessions the audit reports the recorded cost as the comparison basis (or an explicit 'recorded cost; not re-priced' note) instead of re-pricing all tokens at current catalog rates; current-estimate re-pricing remains for estimate-only sessions; no regression on the catalog-miss/unknown-model note lanes
- evidence: campaign-recorded in agenttrace ROADMAP.md
- rider selection (2026-10-08, run e88f2da2 prioritize 8fb1bd7c @92149bd, uncommitted): SELECTED as 'honest dollars' rider — same cost-truth mechanism story as the lead, bounded effort, PoC'd; full acceptance in scope; done-flip reserved to the commit gate. Hunk-disjointness census 2026-10-08: this row's seams governance.rs :86-111/:295/:401 vs four unlanded sibling governance deltas (32f3b7a1 :8/:227-239/:381-409, 4c3ca863 :1087 tests, bbde2156 :476/:531, fc996e6d :834) — zero overlap. Compose seam: 32f3b7a1's in-flight clamped_values disclosures on the same cost_audit lane must survive this change; governance-reports.md sentence re-check rides the docs gate if cost_audit fields move.
- EXECUTED (2026-10-08, run e88f2da2 cycle 3 implement 20228c17 → compound 884f0594, pre-review): recorded-cost sessions are now the comparison basis — sessions carrying upstream-recorded cost (upstream_cost_usd) report it instead of catalog re-pricing: row pricing_source 'vendor-recorded cost; not re-priced', new recorded_cost_usd field, pricing_note discloses 'N session(s) carry $X.XXXX vendor-recorded cost; recorded cost is the comparison basis, not re-priced at current rates', top-level current_estimated_cost_usd sums the recorded basis; estimate-only rows still re-price at current catalog rates exactly as before (the false-drift 'estimated cost changed since ingestion… would now recalculate a different total' note fires ONLY on estimate-only rows). Pinned red-first by cost_audit_recorded_cost_is_the_comparison_basis and cost_audit_estimate_only_rows_still_reprice_at_current_rates; live replay of the de96d4cc PoC recipe rebuilt at this base (the original /tmp corpus was swept): pi standalone-usage entry, cost.total 0.07 on 38,500 in / 950 out claude-sonnet-4-5 → stored==current==recorded 0.07, drift note ABSENT, pricing_source + note + recorded_cost_usd all disclosed (was stored 0.07 vs re-priced 0.1298 + false-drift note). Docs: governance-reports.md Cost-audit section carries the recorded-basis sentence. Gates: targeted 575/0, full workspace 575/0 / 29 suites, fmt/clippy/deny/14 CI rc0. RESIDUAL SEAMS discovered live during the PoC replays (next-cycle mint candidates, compound banner; NOT this row's acceptance): (a) pi MESSAGE-INLINE usage.cost.total is dropped by the parser (extracts only from standalone type:"usage" entries — parser.rs :2132 message arm vs :1925/:2041 standalone arm), so the recorded-basis contract does not reach the inline shape; (b) the sqlite lane's Metrics reconstruction drops upstream_cost_usd, so audit over sqlite-aggregated sessions (incl. 'multiple models' rows) is recorded-cost-blind.
- REVIEW-FIXED (2026-10-08, run e88f2da2 independent_review:fix 8dc257fc, review 9af53c3f F1): the pre-registered 'mixed sessions' evidence arm had landed nothing and the EXECUTED note did not disclose the chosen semantics — the row basis is AGGREGATE: one recorded session (or even one recorded usage entry) flips the whole (provider, model) row onto the recorded basis (estimated_cost_usd = the stored sum, no catalog re-price, no drift note for estimate-only members). Semantics KEPT (dollars stay honest — no double-pricing) but now disclosed by name and pinned: the note names the split — 'N of M session(s) carry $X.XXXX vendor-recorded cost; … (K estimate-only session(s) and $Y.YYYY of the stored total estimated at ingest included, not separately re-priced)' — and pure recorded rows keep the original note verbatim. FIRST-HAND CORRECTION of the review's live reading: mixed-row-poc.jsonl parses to ONE session, not '1 recorded + 1 estimate-only session' — the pi journal merges both usage streams into a single session carrying recorded $0.07 inside a $13.57 stored total, so the live mixed shape is WITHIN-SESSION, which is why the split is named in DOLLARS as well as sessions. Pinned by cost_audit_mixed_row_names_the_split_and_keeps_the_recorded_basis (2-session row) and cost_audit_recorded_session_with_estimate_at_ingest_portion_names_the_usd_split (the live single-session shape, full-note equality). Member-level re-pricing of the estimate-only minority stays the recorded next-cycle candidate (the compound banner's parser residual (a) — message-inline cost dropped — is the manufacturing source).
- integrated (2026-10-14, conflict case 800cf090, merge of run e88f2da2 candidate 217783f into HEAD past base 92149bd): SUPERSEDED-MECHANICS ADJUDICATION — HEAD's landed rm-520/rm-578 same-basis contract (650e9c2 + 4446959, ancestors of HEAD but NOT of the candidate base) wins the mechanics over this run's "never re-price a recorded row" rule, which would additionally hide genuine catalog drift on the unrecorded half of a mixed row; the landed basis math already prices recorded classes exactly once and withholds the re-price entirely when the recorded token split is not reproducible. This run's DISCLOSURE CONTRACT landed on top: recorded_cost_usd on --audit rows and --audit <session>, review-9af53c3f-F1 aggregate split naming in pricing_note ("N of M session(s) carry $X.XXXX vendor-recorded cost ($Y.YYYY of the stored total estimated at ingest)"), the session-level "rides the comparison at face value" note, and the split named beside the rm-578 withholding on split-lost rows; estimate-only rows still re-price at current rates. Pins re-anchored on the merged seam (the dated EXECUTED/REVIEW-FIXED lines above cite the pre-integration names): cost_audit_recorded_row_reprices_on_the_recorded_basis, cost_audit_estimate_only_rows_still_reprice_at_current_rates, cost_audit_mixed_row_names_the_split_and_withholds_the_reprice, cost_audit_recorded_row_with_estimate_at_ingest_dollars_names_the_split, session_cost_audit_recorded_cost_rides_the_comparison_at_face_value.

### SQLite snapshot poisoning: a failed DB open stores an EMPTY snapshot that later masks healthy databases
- id: `rm-626` | track: correctness | priority: 72.0 | status: implemented
- signals: correctness.cache_poisoning:crates/agenttrace-core/src/sqlite_sessions.rs::L165 (load_hermes_sqlite_sessions stores whatever the query returned — and query_hermes_sqlite_sessions returns Vec::new() when open_sqlite_read_only fails, :170-172) + ::L249 (opencode twin; query_opencode_sqlite_sessions fail arm :253-255, same shape) + ::L150-155 (open_sqlite_read_only sets no busy_timeout, so even a transient SQLITE_BUSY becomes a silent total failure that then gets cached)
- acceptance: a failed or unreadable DB never persists an empty snapshot — store_sqlite_snapshot is skipped (or stores a failure sentinel that load treats as a miss) whenever the query failed; a chmod-000 → chmod-644 sequence recovers on the NEXT run (parsed >= 1 once permissions return) instead of reporting zero sessions forever; a regression test drives store-empty-after-failed-open → permissions restored → load re-parses, red-first on the current binary; busy_timeout (or an equivalent retry policy) is pinned by test for concurrent-writer contention; --overview -f json never reports 0 sessions for a home whose DBs are readable but were briefly locked
- evidence: assess a6790edb PoC F1 (HOME=/tmp/at-assess-a679/home1, --overview -f json x3: chmod 000 → empty Vec stored AND cached; chmod 644 → still 0 sessions served from the poisoned snapshot; wiping the cache → parsed:1); code walk sqlite_sessions.rs:103-143 (plural DB sources share the load/store pair), :150-155, :165/:249, :170-186/:206-207, :253-255; session_cache.rs:434-489 (snapshot load/store), :606-615 (FileFingerprint mtime/size/wal/shm)
- notes: adjacent to rm-292 (schema-mismatch cache-restore) but a different defect class — failure-to-miss, not failure-to-invalidate; lands with or after rm-627's per-file keying (under the current single slot a failing store also clobbers a sibling DB's healthy snapshot)
- notes: compound c2 2026-10-07 (run a0d0d353 prioritize 59dcf6de) — DESIGNATED cycle-2 implementation batch LEAD, paired with rm-627 (same lane, one change-unit). Selected over every alternative on: impact (silent total-session loss PERSISTED across runs — truthfulness class), risk (LOW-MED: bounded to sqlite_sessions.rs + session_cache.rs + tests, no pricing/report semantics), effort (S-M: PoC-driven sequences already scripted at /tmp/at-assess-a679/home1-home3), dependencies (none; rm-626 guard first-or-together per the ordering note above), strategic value (fork-FIRST — upstream master carries the same name-keyed slot and empty-store shape, verified via git show upstream/master). COORDINATION: sibling ff0068ca's unlanded rm-596 ingestion-honesty band touches the same load/store call sites (SqliteIngestReport unreadable/dropped-rows disclosure) — compose, don't replace: its unreadable-DB signal is the natural predicate for this row's skip-store guard; integration resolves the textual overlap per house precedent. Fresh claim census at selection: rm-626/627 still unclaimed (fleet ceiling note 'rm-627 @ run-a0d0d353, next free ≥ rm-628' carried by 460d0633's banner); workbuddy #311 matrix NOT selectable (multi-claimed: 99d1c79c + aa31de94 + e5653f52/91833f02 #312 bands); assess K1/K2/K3 claimed (460d0633/9873fc06/c762c2b8).
- implemented (2026-10-07, compound c2 run a0d0d353): pre-review/uncommitted at 8991144 in worktree run-a0d0d3537145-a0d0d353 — guard-first change-unit with rm-627 (one seam, both rows): query_hermes/opencode_sqlite_sessions now return Option (None = read failed, store skipped), open_sqlite_read_only gains busy_timeout, and the opencode leg adds a sqlite_master prepare guard (garbage non-sqlite files previously flowed through as empty and were stored); red-first: failed_hermes_read_never_persists_or_serves_an_empty_snapshot FAILED pre-fix ('a failed hermes read must not persist any snapshot'); implement 764dedd8, targeted_tests 98b9a2f9 RC=0 323/0, full_tests 257ca470 RC=0 466/0 + 21-lane CI mirror, conductor digest validation:v1:a72443d2… — done-flip reserved for the commit gate after independent review; cycle record docs/stewardship/2026-10-07-cycle2-compound-record-runa0d0d353.md.
- review-fix (2026-10-07, d48c5af7 after independent_review 7d51a912 NEEDS_CHANGES): M1 contention pin LANDED — busy_timeout_waits_out_a_short_lock_and_fails_closed_without_storing_on_a_long_one (sqlite_sessions.rs tests) drives a real BEGIN EXCLUSIVE writer against hermes_state_db_fixture: a 100ms short lock is waited out (load succeeds, 2 sessions, snapshot persisted), a lock held across the load fails closed to zero sessions with NO snapshot persisted, and release recovers — the acceptance clause's [pin: a targeted contention test locking {db}, asserting the loader waits out a SHORT lock and fails closed (never persisting) on a LONG one] bracket is now satisfied by an in-tree test.
- review-fix (2026-10-07, ca0ba058 re-review hardening d48c5af7's pin): the M1 test was MUTATION-PROVEN VACUOUS — deleting open_sqlite_read_only's busy_timeout call left it GREEN (waited-run 20.35s vs 2.84s), because rusqlite 0.32.1 opens every connection with a silent default sqlite3_busy_timeout(db, 5000) (inner_connection.rs:119): a short lock is waited out either way and the long-lock leg only stalls ~20s instead of ~1s, with no timing assertion to see it. Hardened with leg 0: the test reads `pragma busy_timeout` off the connection open_sqlite_read_only returns and asserts it equals SQLITE_BUSY_TIMEOUT (250ms) — re-mutation now fails deterministically (left: 5000, right: 250, 0.08s red; green after byte-exact restore), so a silent fallback to the 5s default can no longer ship. Behavioral legs (short-lock wait-out / long-lock fail-closed-no-store / release-recovery) unchanged.
- notes (2026-10-07, review 7d51a912 L1, LOW / no action required): concurrent multi-process ledger writes can drop each other's entry (unlocked read-modify-write at the store path, session_cache.rs); single-run self-healing last-write-wins semantics, same write class as the pre-existing single-slot write — accepted as-is.

### statusline_insights limit-crossings loop is O(n*k) with a JSON clone+parse per probe — sparse journals degrade quadratically
- id: `rm-921` | track: reliability | priority: 70.0 | status: implemented (pre-review; full-row provenance combined at integration: acceptance (a)-(e) — the crossings sweep — landed with run 12f4e5fb via integration merge e9cb9c1, that flip was normalized away by the 2026-10-11 00:21 managed render and is restored here; run e4eb2254's EOF-edge residual rider lands the remaining arm — both legs are in the tree, bullets below)
- acceptance: (a) single precomputation pass parses each capture's window state once; crossings derived by a sorted sweep (O(n log n) or better) — no per-probe from_value; (b) boundary before/after selected by captured_at (max-before/min-after); (c) red-first sparse-shape regression test pinning the bound (wall-time or step-count proxy) proven red against unfixed source per the red-first provenance rule; (d) --statusline-report / --doctor / TUI governance outputs byte-identical on the benign corpus before vs after; (e) full suite green
- evidence: campaign-recorded in agenttrace ROADMAP.md
- residual observation (2026-10-10, run e4eb2254 cycle 1, assess 285d2fce @ e9cb9c1): read_message_line's EOF branch tests line.is_empty() BEFORE overlong, so an overlong unterminated line whose final chunk exactly triggers the buffer clear is silently swallowed — live: exactly 1048577 bytes no-newline → empty output, clean exit, NO refusal; 1048577+8192 → the disclosed refusal. Fix shape: one branch reorder (check overlong at EOF too); A/B corpus preserved under /tmp/at-assess-e4eb.
- residual fixed 2026-10-10 (run e4eb2254 cycle 1, implement 3b613f9a, pre-review, riding the rm-947 batch): the EOF arm now consults `overlong` before `line.is_empty()` (mcp.rs read_message_line) — a cap-crossing unterminated final line reports MessageLine::Overlong instead of Eof. Red-first by temp-swap on pristine source: overlong_line_terminated_by_eof_is_refused_not_silently_dropped fails with ZERO responses; live on the fixed binary: exactly 1048577 bytes no-newline now yields the -32600 transport-cap refusal (id null) with exit 0. Framing preserved: rm-923's newline-terminated drain test stays green (mcp_server 7/0).
- integration seam (2026-10-11, conflict case acdfd9c5ec4f404292b4b08c0df773fe): the residual-fixed mechanics above were authored against the pre-rm-782 `read_message_line` reader; the landed rm-782 byte-level `read_wire_line` already orders the EOF arm correctly (`oversized` before emptiness), so the rider lands here as the red-first EOF-edge pin `overlong_line_terminated_by_eof_is_refused_not_silently_dropped` in tests/mcp_server.rs, asserting the landed rm-923 `-32600` transport-cap contract.
- residual (2026-10-13, run 4401a3808865434ba82d7c0b6f896c3c cycle 2, assess 1ab591e2353b49288794304b81c39451 @ecf84dc): the once-per-capture discipline this row pins has one live violator — capture.context_window() is parsed TWICE per capture in statusline_insights (statusline.rs:1004, the peak fold, and statusline.rs:1016, the per-session loop), while rate_limit/spend_limit honor the pre-parsed windows/spends vecs this row added; each call re-clones and serde-parses the context_window subtree, and the n·k parse blowup is exactly the failure class this row exists to prevent. Fix shape: pre-parse Option<StatuslineContextWindow> once per capture and feed both consumers.
- residual-closed 2026-10-13 (compound 248535db, run 4401a3808865434ba82d7c0b6f896c3c cycle 2, repository-maintenance d7437b55bdf242ceabaa02fec04510cf @ ecf84dc), riding the 'full-clone-green' batch: the statusline violator is closed — capture.context_window() parsed ONCE per capture (shared pre-parse feeding both the filter_map peak fold and the per-session loop); red-first counter test context_window_parsed_once_per_capture panicked left:4/right:2 on pristine code, green with count==2 + peak==Some(90.0) after; targeted core --lib 367/0 + full 2d4586fc 1048/0 rc0; status stays implemented (pre-review)

### agenttrace doctor must validate the positional scan root
- id: `rm-1012` | track: correctness | priority: 65.0 | status: implemented (2026-10-12 run 975ac79d cycle 3 implement d2ac1763 @ 82e23b9, in-worktree uncommitted; done-flip reserved to the commit gate, rm-012 convention)
- acceptance: the `(None, Some(p))` doctor arm (main.rs:592-593) routes the positional through `validate_explicit_dir` (or an equivalent exists + is_dir check) BEFORE any scan or render — `agenttrace doctor /nonexistent-dir-xyz` and `agenttrace doctor /etc/passwd` exit rc=2 with refusal wording naming the positional (the rm-608 failure class), never rc=0 reporting success with 0 sessions; the `--dir` arm's behavior stays byte-identical; the both-given refusal stays; a contract test pins both refusal shapes (nonexistent path + file-valued path) beside the existing --dir guards.
- evidence: live PoC at 82e23b9 (assess daee6149 F2, poc-transcripts.txt): both commands exit rc=0 reporting healthy scans with 0 sessions; main.rs:578-596 dispatch — the --dir arm validates via rm-608's helper (main.rs:1805-1818, exits 2 on missing/non-directory) while the positional arm returns `Some(PathBuf::from(p))` raw; rm-608's in-code comment (:586-590) names this exact class ("a typo'd or file-valued directory reported rc=0 'healthy'"); landed lineage: the 2026-10-10 positional-scope batch (wall banner, run 57c491338818, seam a4ff3f6c/a085b2c3) resolved the dispatch holding rm-608 on the --dir arm and scan-root on the match — validation never reached the positional arm.
- compound c3 EXECUTED (2026-10-12 implement d2ac1763 @ 82e23b9, in-worktree uncommitted; targeted 860a6043 + full c31a2d82 recorded green — consumed at compound, not re-run): new `validate_positional_dir(p)` (main.rs:1845, kept a SIBLING of rm-608's `validate_explicit_dir` — not a parameter — so the --dir arm's bytes stay untouched) guards the `(None, Some(p))` dispatch arm (:602) before any scan or render: `doctor /nonexistent-dir-xyz` → rc=2 "session directory does not exist" + guidance naming the positional scan root; `doctor /etc/passwd` → rc=2 "positional scan root is not a directory" (both were rc=0 "healthy, 0 sessions" pre-fix — assess F2 red re-confirmed live before the edit); --dir arm verified BYTE-IDENTICAL (cmp on pre/post stderr); both-given refusal stays rc=1; --demo + positional still hits the demo-source guard first. Pin: `doctor_positional_root_refuses_missing_and_file_valued_paths` (doctor_demo_contract 3/0; entrypoints 53/0, launch_guards 13/0).

- acceptance: `agenttrace watch [paths]` tails session files on append, re-analyzes only the changed file incrementally, and re-renders the live overview/sessions view (cost so far, waste signals, loop detection); implementation stays file-tail re-analysis of written events and never stream interception, honoring the recorded non-goal; cost stays linear in changed files via rm-023's index or a per-file cache; the notify dependency is evaluated against the static-build profile

### Upstream-status labels conflate absent-probe with registry failure and rank unknown authority as least-fresh
- id: `rm-052` | track: reliability | priority: 58.0 | status: candidate
- acceptance: npm channel state distinguishes curl-absent from HTTP failure from unparseable payload; authority ranking presents unknown explicitly rather than as least-fresh; JSON schema changes additive only
- evidence: campaign-recorded in agenttrace ROADMAP.md
- evidence refresh (2026-10-10, run e4eb2254 cycle 1, assess 285d2fce @ e9cb9c1 — upstream.rs:547-558 re-read): the npm probe remains TIME-bounded only, never byte-bounded; live proof with the EXACT argv (curl -fsSL --max-time 15) against a local chunked server: 390,084,000 bytes delivered in 1.5s, rc=0 (~4GB reachable inside the 15s window from a fast local/MITM endpoint), fully buffered by Command::output() before from_utf8_lossy + serde copies. Hardening menu unchanged from this row's residual (piped bounded read + kill, or the disclosed ureq clause); any future 'byte-bounded' claim must carry the Content-Length-present caveat.
- sibling-lane extension (2026-10-11, run b171537d4e3d4e1ebe8904ee2ccdb1fb, roadmap 61636bb689b747d98e0f793745a844a0, consuming assess 785b4707 @dbc5b76): the npm probe's byte ceiling landed with run c23d7ee8 (NPM_REGISTRY_MAX_BYTES = 256 KiB, upstream.rs:574, enforced our-side of the pipe via read_capped :640 + process-group kill, threat-model doc :563-573) — but the GIT lane, the sibling that shares the drain machinery, still has no byte bound: spawn_drain (upstream.rs:205-217) pumps both pipes with unbounded read_to_end inside the abandonable thread, fetch_remote runs `git fetch --quiet` under UPSTREAM_FETCH_TIMEOUT=30s (upstream.rs:412, const :43) and git_bounded probes run 10s (upstream.rs:427-429, const :42) — deadline-bounded only (that half is rm-731's, healthy in-tree: both pins live at upstream.rs:1038 + governance.rs:1899), never byte-bounded. Vector: `git fetch --quiet` still relays server-side sideband ("remote:") text on stderr, so a hostile or MITM'd remote controls unbounded bytes through git's pipe into our drains — memory grows unbounded within the deadline, exactly the class the npm cap closed for a hostile registry. Acceptance for the git arm: a capped spawn_drain variant (or read_capped composed into the drain threads) mirroring NPM_REGISTRY_MAX_BYTES discipline with its own GIT_*_MAX_BYTES consts, fail-closed on over-cap, unit tests pinning an over-cap drain truncates+kills rather than growing. INTEGRATOR NOTE: the landed cap's doc comment cites this row's numeral while the row's own subject above is the older npm-channel-truthfulness lane — reconcile the subprocess-drain-bound family by title at the next landing (rm-731 owns the deadline half, this rider claims the git byte half).
- EXECUTED (2026-10-11, run b171537d4e3d4e1ebe8904ee2ccdb1fb, implement e7beab2392954ded8afa48bab337ee9e @dbc5b76, pending commit gate): git arm landed — UPSTREAM_GIT_MAX_BYTES = 256 KiB (sibling of NPM_REGISTRY_MAX_BYTES, both enforced our-side of the pipe, contract-pinned in run_bounded_bounds_are_contract), GitRunError::OverCap{op,cap_bytes} + Display arm, spawn_drain rewritten from read_to_end to chunked reads returning DrainOutcome{bytes,exceeded} (abandonable-send semantics preserved), run_bounded's in-loop try_recv kills the tree the MOMENT a drain sees the overrun (npm-lane discipline) with a completed-under-cap stash so the post-loop recv never blocks; callers fetch_remote + git_bounded probes updated. Tests: run_bounded_kills_a_child_pushing_past_the_byte_cap (1 MiB vs 4 KiB, killed <5 s), run_bounded_returns_output_under_the_byte_cap (happy path), run_bounded_surfaces_a_stderr_overrun_as_over_cap (the sideband vector git actually relays). rm-543/583/731 deadline pins green; full gate 927/0/53 rc0; engine digest validation:v1:44345ab1ea6635afebcfe7be25ed3a1cebfdde395134662fdb21f02b1e932921.

- EXECUTED (2026-10-08, run 749cd29820f3 cycle 1 implement a0ef8a86, adopting reaped 44a88914's verified drift; recorded outcomes, NOT re-run at compound): SUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS = ["2026-07-28", "2025-11-25", "2025-06-18"] (crates/agenttrace-cli/src/mcp.rs:78) negotiated newest-mutual in initialize_result (:367) — never an echo; unknown client versions fall back to newest-supported with a one-line stderr disclosure (:372-373); locked by protocol_version_is_negotiated_never_echoed (tests/mcp_server.rs) incl. the bogus-version arm; README/guide state the supported set; PoC replay on the release binary: initialize 1999-99-99 → 2026-07-28 + disclosure (was: echoed verbatim)

The sanitizer is idempotent and tab-honest: 0x09 is layout for the document sanitizer and survives it, while the stricter line sanitizer (status-line cells) replaces it — pinned in BOTH composition orders; markdown_cell/html_escape keep their entity escapes as defense-in-depth with the :2833 merge note made true; the matrix test was RED on the assess corpus before the fix (implement-phase RED-FIRST record: overview markdown 10 ESC/6 BEL pre-fix → 0/0 post)

- compound c2 2026-10-07 (run 614624d7, implement cc7af0fb; recorded pre-review outcomes, not re-run at compound): EXECUTED — validate_sample_applicability + validate_history_applicability (mirroring rm-244 validate_range_applicability) wired in run() right after the range guard: --sample on a non-sampling lane exits 1 naming the flag, the ignoring lane, and the consuming lanes; --include-history/--preserve-history outside their report actions likewise (TUI entry rejected loudly, no more silent no-op); sample_consumed() predicate spans the governance lanes + --compare; --help scoping widened for --sample and ADDED for the history flags. ### SQLite lane claims ASC-ordering semantics on a non-numeric time_created column
- id: `rm-954` | track: correctness | priority: 3 | status: implemented (2026-10-11 compound flip by run e944a060a640 cycle 1 implement 2ba22694, compound 9ea66a38; commit gate pending; minted 2026-10-10 by run e944a060a6404c68a77973063e6ea2b4, attempt 5e585bbb8a4d4391a612a76b8460c07c)
- signals: correctness.ordering_overclaim:crates/agenttrace-core/src/sqlite_sessions.rs::L776 — the query asserts time_created ASC drives latest/earliest selection while the column carries RFC3339 TEXT; SQLite sorts NULL first and text after numerics, so a wrong-typed or NULL time_created row reorders the window (assess cycle-1 F6, run e944a060a640 assess bdbd50cb @8982722)
- acceptance: (a) either no ordering semantics are asserted on the raw TEXT column (drop the claim) or selection sorts on a normalized/epoch expression; (b) a fixture mixing numeric-text and NULL time_created values proves the selected latest/earliest row is invariant to insertion order; (c) clean RFC3339-only corpora render byte-identical before vs after the change (golden tests)
- evidence: sqlite3 CLI differential (ORDER BY time_created ASC over mixed NULL/numeric/text values) demonstrating the ordering quirk; red/green twin corpus pre/post fix; fmt + clippy + full suite green at the fix HEAD
- evidence refresh (2026-10-12, run 4309a268453f assess e81f23a0 F6 @ b798247): the ordering-overclaim has a second arm — sqlite_value_asc_rank (sqlite_sessions.rs:1334-1347) returns (i32,i64) and SqliteValue::Real(n) => (1, n as i64) TRUNCATES the fraction, so two REAL time_created values with distinct fractions (epoch …89.1 vs …89.9) collapse to equal ranks and resolve by rowid where SQLite ASC would order by fraction — wrong earliest-user-part (session name) selection on fractional-epoch corpora; the :864-866 comment claims storage-class-faithful ordering. Acceptance rider: keep the REAL fraction in the rank key (or narrow the claim); a fixture with fractional REAL epochs red/greens the fix.
- notes: no wall row owned this seam at mint time (grep time_created/ASC: zero def-row hits — only prose inside other rows' evidence); adjacent but disjoint rows: the rm-history same-second collision fold and the --range admission row both read time_created but do not own the ordering claim; LOW severity — fires only under pathological column typing

### Legacy tag-marker gap makes the plugin-version gate red in any full clone
- id: `rm-1064` | track: reliability | priority: 72.0 | status: implemented
- acceptance: staged, independently shippable: (a) `bash scripts/ci/check-plugin-version.sh` exits 0 in a clone that carries the FULL origin tag set (73 version tags; ci.yml:83-86 already fetches tags before running the gate), by giving every merged tag lacking a CHANGELOG section a single-line `<!-- no-changelog-section: vX.Y.Z: reason -->` marker — 50 tags today (v0.0.4 and v0.3.0..v0.3.48); the 8 existing markers only cover v0.7.2+; (b) the CHANGELOG diff is additive-only (one dated block, zero existing bytes change) and the markers' reasons match the recorded enumeration; (c) red-first: deleting one marker in a scratch copy flips the gate rc 0→1 and restoring it flips back; (d) a fresh full clone (or the canonical checkout after `git fetch --tags`) runs the lane green.
- evidence: assess 1ab591e2353b49288794304b81c39451 (2026-10-13) — `bash scripts/ci/check-plugin-version.sh` → EXIT=1 'merged tag v0.0.4 has no CHANGELOG section' at ecf84dc; marker-census loop over `git tag --merged HEAD`: 50 tags without section or marker; `git ls-remote --tags origin` → 73 version-shaped tags incl. v0.0.4/v0.3.x; dossier /tmp/at-assess-1ab5/adversarial-assessment-dossier.md (reproduction one-liner included).
- notes: the markers were last extended 2026-10-07 (CHANGELOG.md:359-360, compound c2 banner) for the then-current tag surface; the canonical clone (created 2026-10-12) acquired the FULL historical tag set, which is what turns this lane red on every push/cron and fresh clone — the only affected gate lane is 'Codex plugin version sync'.

### Budget truth: pre-journal session spend must not land in the first captured day

### npm launcher erases the binary's exit-code contract at the process boundary
- id: `rm-1438` | track: developer-experience | priority: 48.0 | status: implemented (minted-implemented 2026-10-15, run c1cafed561834ed19e6e05fa154e1be3 cycle 5 implement 668492c3 + compound 7676255e; done-flip reserved to the commit gate, rm-012 convention)
- signals: dx.launcher_signal_collapse:npm/bin/agenttrace.js::L30 (spawnSync result handled with process.exit(result.status ?? 1) — a signal death reports status:null plus the signal name in result.signal, which the ?? arm discards, collapsing SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP deaths into generic rc 1 at the npm-exec boundary so shells and CI lose the 130/143 conventions). STANDING CORRECTION (stewardship ff47386a, re-verified against the code at 3e90993 before minting): the launcher does NOT coerce numeric exit codes — `result.status ?? 1` passes a numeric rc through unchanged; the assess phrasing overclaimed. The verified gap is exactly the signal path.
- acceptance: (1) numeric exit codes pass through the wrapper verbatim (0 stays 0, rc N stays N); (2) a child killed by a signal exits the wrapper with that signal's semantics (re-raise to self, 128+signum as the fallback when a handler interferes); (3) spawn-based contract tests pin both arms, red-first on HEAD (recorded: pre-fix launcher vs SIGINT stub -> status:1 signal:null)
- evidence: assess 68fdc77d F6 + stewardship ff47386a correction + prioritize 93789f24 (developer-experience 48.0; numeral TRIPLE-CLAIMED in fleet fog — identity = this title + run c1cafed5, reconcile by title never numeral); batchable with rm-1368 (the npm CLI lane row) but must_remain_separate — distinct acceptance surfaces
- EXECUTED (2026-10-15, run c1cafed561834ed19e6e05fa154e1be3 cycle 5 implement 668492c3, pre-review): numeric statuses exit verbatim; signal deaths re-raise to the wrapper's own pid (process.kill(process.pid, result.signal)) with a 128+signal-number map fallback for overridden handlers; the honest-red proof was recorded at implement time (HEAD launcher vs SIGINT stub -> status:1 signal:null; fixed -> signal re-raised). VALIDATED (consumed at compound 7676255e, not re-run): targeted fd91a652 — npm lane 8/8 (numeric exit codes pass through the wrapper unchanged / success exit code zero passes through / SIGINT death is re-raised, not converted to exit 1 / SIGTERM death is re-raised); full 1a9de0d3 — npm test lane green inside the verbatim ci.yml battery.
